       FOREWORD
           While designing BESM Fourth Edition for its relaunch, I had a philosophy in mind that I believed would be
       vital to the game line’s success: keep it simple and approachable. The foundational core rules needed to reflect the
       very best that anime and manga role-playing had to offer. To meet this immense expectation, I couldn’t burden
       BESM with too many rules. The Tri-Stat System became larger and more complex as it moved from BESM 1e, to
       BESM 2e, to Silver Age Sentinels, to Tri-Stat dX, to BESM 3e – and I wanted BESM 4e to break this trend. The
       character options during creation would remain comprehensive, but I would streamline the game rules and only
       include what was essential to tell great stories.
           This would leave many details out of BESM Fourth Edition, though, and I knew that a sizeable portion of
       gamers liked the extra crunch that more precise rules offered. Not just from a power-gamer perspective, of course,
       but also with an eye towards more dramatic narratives. What if a character wanted to push the magic potion out
       of the wizard’s hand with a telekinetic blast? Or sit motionless on the hilltop for many minutes, staring through
       the laser rifle scope, to ensure their target wouldn’t get the opportunity to complete the demonic summoning
       ritual? Or hold their Initiative action until the entire team was ready to combine their elemental attacks into one
       overwhelming effort? The BESM Fourth Edition core rules are sufficiently robust to allow for such actions – but
       wouldn’t it be better if gaming groups had a set of rules to make this process easier?
           BESM Extras was my solution to ensuring simplicity while retaining flexibility.
           I don’t consider BESM Extras to be a role-playing game expansion in the traditional sense. Rather, I see it
       more as a second core rulebook companion that was bundled as its own individual product; BESM 4B, if you will.
       Inside these pages you will find a wealth of extra rules and details for your anime and manga adventures that can
       be categorised into several primary areas:
           Extended Choices – Additional Enhancements and Limiters for Attributes; new Defects; mechanical tweaks to
       existing offerings; expansions on the numbers of Levels and Ranks; etc.
           Core Enhancements – Baseline values to reflect shock (for gritty games) and mental sanity (for horror games).
           Alternative Offerings – The opportunity to use distinct individual Skills rather than bundled Skill Groups,
       including opportunities to vary Skill costs by genre.
PAGE
  2
           Expanded Mechanics – Tactical actions; diverse combat manoeuvres; grappling mechanics; enhanced defence
       rules; guidance for using Attributes in combat; pushing powers; combined attacks; morale checks for NPCs;
       innovative rules that address mass combat on the battlefield and social combat at gala events; non-combat damage;
       expanded injury details.
           Optional Rules – Critical hits and failures; margins of success; knockback; range penalties; alternate Stat costs;
       standardised character creation assignments; dynamic Initiative; giving players access to retroactive continuity or
       “retcon”; variable damage; running BESM as a diceless game.
           Power Applications – Power Packs, which are groupings of Limiters and Enhancements; Power Bundles, which
       are pre-built collections of Attributes that are analogous to magical spells or spell-like effects.
           Adventuring Elements – Poisons; disease; opponent Threat Scores; high-level character guidance; artificial
       intelligence considerations; firearm and vehicle customisation.
           Creative Examples – Adventuring gear; items of power; mecha and vehicles; protective devices; weapons.

           There is an enormous amount of gamer resources presented in BESM Extras, which ensures that there’s something
       for everyone in this companion volume. Take as much as you like for your adventures, modify aspects to better suit
       the campaign needs for your group, use the examples to help you build your own alternatives, and discard the rest.
           Thank you so much for once again granting me a seat at your gaming table.




                                                                                                         Mark MacKinnon
                                                                                                             August 2020

CORE GAME
FRAMEWORK
  01: CORE FRAMEWORK
BROADENING YOUR GAME
   Welcome to BESM Extras – the ultimate resource for rules variations, extended game mechanics, expanded optional
guidelines, and plenty of ready-to-use examples.
   BESM Fourth Edition presents a comprehensive collection of essential core character options, including 86 Attributes,
130 Enhancements and Limiters, and 36 Defects. This chapter expands that list by including additional options that can
enhance your gameplay. Several of the alternatives can be assigned to any character, and the text indicates which others
should only be applied when your game adventures incorporate specific optional rules that are detailed in later chapters.
   As with all things BESM, the GM and player group can decide together which elements to use and which to ignore.

                                                                      KNOCKBACK
                                                                          Only assign this Enhancement in games using
                                                                      the Knockback optional rule (page 58). The attack
                                                                      impacts the target forcefully, knocking them back one
                                                                      metre for every point of damage inflicted. The Armour
                                                                      Attribute is ignored when determining Knockback
                                                                      distance; Force Fields without the Impacting Limiter
                                                                      do reduce Knockback, however. This Enhancement is
                                                                      primarily for Weapons with high kinetic energy, and
                                                                      is not typically assigned to slashing, piercing, or other
                                                                      non-impacting attacks. The Weapon’s final Knockback
                                                                      distance is doubled each time this Enhancement is
                                                                      assigned – after all modifiers are considered (such as the
                                                                      Enhanced Knockback Combat Technique, page 5, and
                                                                      numerous damage modifiers).

  01: CORE FRAMEWORK
SANITY POINTS
    For gritty horror games and paranormal/occult
adventures that explore the ephemeral nature of reality
and humanity, characters should also calculate an
additional Derived Value (see BESM4, page 169) called
Sanity Points.
    Sanity Points represent a character’s relationship
with reality and conscious understanding of the greater
world. Traumatic situations that befall a character can
subtract from their current number of Sanity Points,
as can revelations that strain the fabric of a character’s
perceived existence. Once a character’s Sanity Points fall
to 5 or below, their grasp on lucidity and rationality
begins to falter as their mind slips into eventual madness.
    Your character’s base number of Sanity Points is
equal to the sum of the Mind Stat and Soul Stat. Increase
Sanity Points by +2 for every Level of the Unassailable
Attribute (page 9). Reduce Sanity Points by -2 for every
-1 Rank of the Unsettled Defect (page 11).

  uSANITY ROLLS
    Whenever a character is confronted by a traumatic
experience or otherwise perceives an odd event that
challenges their understanding of reality, the GM may
ask for a Sanity roll. Similar to a Stat roll, the success of                                                                         PAGE
                                                                                                                                       15
a Sanity roll is determined by rolling two dice and then
adding the average (rounded down) of the character’s
Mind and Soul Stats. The result is the total roll, which
is compared to a Target Number (see BESM4; page
177) to determine if the task was successful.
CONSIDERATION FOR SKILLS
   The GM may allow certain individual Skills
presented in BESM Extras (page 20) to apply to the
Sanity roll when appropriate – such as the Cultural
Arts, Occult, or Religion Skills, depending on the
potential traumatic encounter – which parallels the
game mechanics of a Skill roll (BESM4, page 179).

CHAPTER 2

SKILLS
                                                                       02: SKILLS
BEYOND SKILL GROUPS
  This section is provided as an alternative to the Skill Groups Attribute (BESM4, page 120), since some players and
Game Masters may prefer a more granular option that reduces bundling of acquired knowledge and talent.
    Your character’s Skills represent their extensive training and experience in a particular field. Each Skill can be thought of as
a sub-Attribute with certain special rules applying to them. A character does not need to have any Skills, but they can be very
useful during adventures, and are a good way to distinguish the character (especially in lower-powered games).
   Instead of the Skill Groups Attribute, assign the Skills Attribute (page 8) to your character during their initial creation or
subsequent advancement to acquire one or more Skills at various Ranks.


    The different Skills are divided into Ranks from                   Rank 1    Developing – The character is gaining
1 through 6, but may optionally go higher with GM                                proficiency with the Skill through regular
permission. Increasing the value of a Skill by one Rank                          application
requires 1-3 Skill Points (provided by the Skills Attribute)           Rank 2 Trained – The character has moderate
depending on the Skill.                                                          training and practice with the Skill
                                                                       Rank 3 Expert – The character has significant
    The higher the Skill Rank, the better your character                         training and practice with the Skill
is and the broader their knowledge within the field.                   Rank 4 Veteran – The character has extensive
Characters with high Stats can be exceptionally proficient                       training and practice with the Skill
within a field even without high Skill Ranks, though;                  Rank 5 Master – The character has exhaustive
they are considered “naturals.” Characters typically have a                      training and practice with the Skill
couple of Levels of the Skills Attribute and thus only have            Rank 6 Grand Master – The character has
a few specific Skills assigned. In a lower-powered Sub-                          unparalleled ability and knowledge with
Human or Human level game – such as one focussed on                              the Skill

 CHAPTER 3

EXPANDED
MECHANICS
                                 03: MECHANICS+
ENHANCING YOUR RULES
    This chapter goes into more detail regarding additional combat choices, and also provides enhanced rules that touch
upon a variety of sections, including: edges and obstacles, pushing powers, hedging and morale for NPCs, social combat,
and more. GMs may wish to use these rules to adjudicate situations that were not addressed in the core BESM rulebook,
or to add a depth of opportunities if combat is an especially important part of campaign adventures.
    Many of the expanded mechanics presented in this chapter interact with other parts of BESM Extras. For example, dice
roll penalties for executing some of the new combat manoeuvres can be reduced or eliminated by assigning select new
Combat Techniques (page 5). Chapter 4 provides a series of optional rules gaming groups can consider as well.

          OPPOSED ROLLS MAY TAKE
          SPILLOVER
              For opposed rolls (such as in
          combat), modification that goes
          beyond a major obstacle or edge
          can apply a reverse modifier to the
          opponent’s roll. For example, if an
          attacker gains both a major edge and
          a minor edge to their roll, only the
          major edge applies and instead a minor
          obstacle is applied to the opposing roll
          (the defender, in this example). Simply
          max out one roll at a major edge or
          obstacle and apply any secondary
          modifiers to the opposing roll as the
          opposite effect.

          ALLOW REROLLS

USING MULTIPLE ROLLS INSTEAD
   If the GM wants to prolong the drama of a mass
battle scene and perhaps turn it into the focus of an
entire game session, they can instead require one army to
win two, three, or more opposed combat rolls. Between
each roll, the GM should allow the players to influence
the narrative of the combat story by describing the
mid-battle scene and the opportunities for meaningful
character interactions that arise.

  uFLOW OF COMBAT TIME
    Unlike regular skirmish-level conflict that operates
within a timeframe of 3-4 second combat rounds, mass
combat unfolds across a much larger time scale. Each
application of mass battle is different and there isn’t a set
rule to indicate precisely how long a combat lasts. The
GM is encouraged to select a reasonable time scale for
the battle, which considers the size of the armies on the
field (dozens? hundreds? thousands? more?) as well as
the scope of their conflict (minor conflict, major battle,
or epic campaign?). Suggested time scales include:
 » Hours for small battles of minor scope
 » Days for small battles of major scope
 » Weeks for large battles of minor scope
 » Months for large battles of major scope
                                                                 PAGE
 » Seasons for epic battles of major scope                        53

 » Years for legendary battles of primal scope
IMPACT OF COMBAT SUCCESS MARGIN
   The GM may also decide to have the margin of
success during the armies’ opposed combat rolls impact
the length of time that the mass battle endures. The
margin of success (see page 58) is the difference between
the winning army’s and defeated army’s total rolls.
 » Slight Success: battle drags on dramatically
 » Moderate Success: time increased considerably
 » Significant Success: time scale as anticipated
 » Major Success: time decreased considerably
 » Extreme Success: battle ends quickly                             EX
                                                                 PANDED

  uMULTIPLE ARMIES
    These mass engagement rules were written for the
typical combat with two opposing armies, but they can
be extrapolated for battles waged on multiple fronts
                                                                        MEC




between numerous armies as well. In these situations,
the GM can either determine the results of each army
pairing separately (ie. like a round-robin tournament
                                                                HANI




style), or can examine the 10 categories across all armies.
They can determine the modifiers that impact the total
                                                                CS




combat rolls for each individual army, and then have
each army make one roll to determine the victor.

 CHAPTER 4

OPTIONAL
 RULES
           04: OPTIONAL RULES
CHANGING THE BASE
    One of the primary philosophies foremost in our thoughts during the creation of BESM Fourth Edition was a desire
to keep the core rules simple and straightforward, and universal in their application. There were already a plethora of
intriguing character choices presented to players – Attributes, Enhancements, Limiters, Defects, and Templates – and
we didn’t want to add too many more. This necessitated the exclusion of several alternative costing preferences and
expanded combat options that we viewed as too diverse and complex (or “crunchy”) to make the cut for the core rules.
   Fortunately, we had already planned to collect these optional rules into BESM Extras, and thus still had the opportunity
to make them available for your anime and manga adventures. Use what you like from this chapter, modify these ideas as
appropriate for your gaming group, and discard what you don’t like.

 CHAPTER 5

EXPLORING
 DAMAGE
05: EXPLORING DAMAGE
EXAMINING THE PAIN
   When most people think of character damage in a role-playing adventure, injuries that are inflicted through combat
probably come to mind. Such base damage guidelines are fully addressed in the BESM core rulebook (BESM4, page 193).
Of course, there are numerous ways a body can be injured outside of combat as well, and this chapter thoughtfully
explores impact damage from crashing and falling, as well as different forms of environmental damage.
    To provide alternatives to the standard fixed damage rules in BESM, Chapter 5 also includes a variety of ways that
attack damage can be modified to increase variety and unpredictability in combat. Finally, guidelines on the next few
pages also examine the roll that shock, unconsciousness, pain, executions, and cinematic recovery can have on your game.

05: EXPLORING DAMAGE
PRESSURE                                                         uENHANCED RECOVERY
    The human body can withstand a wide range of                   In more cinematic and less realistic games, the
pressures (often measured in “atmospheres”), but               rate of recovering Health Points and Energy Points
bones will break and lungs will collapse under extreme         (BESM4, page 195) may proceed too slowly. The GM
conditions. The amount of pressure applied to the body         and players may jointly decide to increase the recovery
is difficult to intuitively estimate unless the character is   rates to hourly instead of daily (for Health Points) or
underwater: pressure increases about one atmosphere for        quarter-hourly instead of hourly (for Energy Points).
every 10 metres of water depth. Characters do not suffer
any significant pressure damage up to 10 atmospheres,              For Weapons with two assignments of the Incurable

400 metres underwater (40 atmospheres), a character
will suffer 30 points of damage each round (40-10=30).
RADIATION
     Harmful radiation, such as that emitted by nuclear
reactors and radioactive chemicals, causes extreme
damage to the human body. The majority of the
damage inflicted by nuclear bombs at or near ground
zero results from heat and shock wave rather than the
nuclear fallout. Exposure to extreme radiation has very
little immediate effect upon a character. Within hours
or days, however, the cellular damage becomes painfully
clear; humans will usually die within a week as the
body shuts down. Low levels of radiation exposure will
eventually cause radiation sickness, cancer, and death,
                                                                                                                           PAGE
but these effects can take months or years to develop.                                                                      71

VACUUM
    A vacuum is a low-pressure area with little or no air to
breathe, such as the vast infinite of space. In anime and
manga reality, the following damage can be experienced
by a character in a vacuum: unconsciousness, frozen
eyeballs, frostbite, boiling blood, and even exploding
limbs. In partial vacuums, such as those artificially
created in Earth laboratories, characters suffer 2-5 points
of damage each round. In near-complete vacuums, such
as outer space, characters suffer 5-10 points of damage
each round.

  uPAIN AND HEALTH POINTS
    Health Points have been abstracted in BESM and
                                                                                                                           EXPLOR

are normally only decreased as a result of Weapon
attacks, environmental damage, and special non-
combat situations. It’s also possible to inflict Health
Point damage that results from bodily pain due to less
obvious injuries, such as pinched nerves, overexertion,
                                                                                                                                  ING D




suboptimal organ function, and more.
    The amount of Health Point damage that the pain
inflicts each hour depends on its severity, ranging from
                                                                                                                          AMAG




1-5 for minor pain to 6-10 for major pain to 11-20
for excruciating pain. Pain damage usually recovers at
the stun damage rate (see BESM4, page 195) but under
                                                                                                                          E




some circumstances may be treated as normal damage.
 CHAPTER 6

PACKS AND
BUNDLES
   06: PACKS & BUNDLES
POWER PACKS
   Power Packs are collections of Limiters and Enhancements that players assign to their character’s Attributes to reflect
the parameters under which the Attributes function. Power Packs are often associated with paranormal origins – divine,
magical, psionic, supernatural, etc. – though they can also represent technological or racial background details. Individual
Power Packs usually reflect one of the character’s single sources of power (BESM4, page 258).
    Six distinct Power Packs are detailed in this section – Crystalist, Divine, Psi, Ritualist, Shamanist, Wizardry – though
players and GMs are encouraged to develop their own named collection of Limiters and Enhancements to best suit their
needs. As normal for BESM, the number printed immediately after the Limiter or Enhancement name indicates how many
times it is assigned to the Attribute in the Power Pack.


WIDE APPLICATION VS. ENHANCEMENTS                               RECORDING POWER PACKS
    Though Power Packs can include both                             Using Power Packs makes recording and reading
Enhancements and Limiters, the examples provided                character sheets easier, since there’s less information to
only include the Potent Enhancement – Area, Duration,           include for each Attribute entry. For example, a druid
Range, and Targets are conspicuously absent. This               with 1 Level of the Control Environment (Darkness)
purposeful choice maintains a universal application of          Attribute with Limiters associated with the Shamanist
the Power Packs, since not all Attributes can have the          Power Pack (effective Level 5) could have this entry
standard Enhancements applied (see BESM4, Table-11:             recorded in two different ways on their character sheet –
Allowable Enhancements, page 146). For example, the             with the second Power Pack entry being more succinct.
Area Enhancement cannot apply to the Plant Control                 1 (5)    Control Environment: Darkness
Attribute, since the Attribute Level itself determines the                  (Emotional +2; Environmental +1; Imbue +1)
area of effect. Similarly, the Range Enhancement cannot            1 (5)    Control Environment: Darkness
apply to Dimension Walk because it is incompatible                          (Shamanist +4)
with how the Attribute functions. Of course, players
can also add Enhancements to custom Power Packs if
the addition better aligns with their character concept.                                                                        PAGE
                                                                                                                                 73
MODIFYING POWER PACKS
    The number of assignments of specific Limiters
(and Potent Enhancement) included in each Power
Pack can be raised or lowered to better match a specific
vision for the Attribute’s use. For example, the Psi Power
Pack includes one assignment of the Deplete Limiter,
indicating that the Attribute burns 10 Energy Points for
each use (for those with immediate effects) or 10 Energy
Points for each minute of use (for those with ongoing
effects). To instead indicate that Attributes associated
with this Power Pack are exceptionally draining on the
character, the Deplete Limiter could alternatively be
assigned three times – now burning 30 Energy Points
for immediate effects or 20 Energy Points/round
for ongoing effects. This modification consequently                                                                                PA
changes the related Attribute’s effective Levels by +8
instead of +6.
                                                                                                                                CKS AN



MODERATING WITH POTENT
    Assigning a collection of Limiters to an Attribute
may quickly escalate its effective Level beyond a desired
                                                                                                                                       D BU




outcome. To rein in a Power Pack’s application on an
Attribute’s effective Level, consider adding multiple
assignments of the Potent Enhancement (BESM4, page
                                                                                                                               NDLE




146), as demonstrated in the Divine, Ritualist, and
Wizardry Power Packs.

          CRYSTALIST
              Right – The Crystalist Power Pack is typically
          assigned to Attributes that tap into the vibrations and
     S
   NDLE




          resonance of natural crystals and grant the mystic the
          power to transform, absorb, amplify, and transmit their
          universal forces. Natural energy is everywhere, and
          crystals are the perfect conduit between dimensions
 ND BU




          and harmonic realities. This Power Pack is appropriate
          for empaths, mystics, occultists, spiritual leaders,
          thaumaturgists, and woke practitioners.
PACKS A




            Power Pack: Crystalist +5
            Delay +1 (A few minutes before Attribute takes effect)
            Detectable +1 (Crystals glows, resonate vibrations)
            Equipment +1 (Requires various crystals)
            Unpredictable +2 (Difficult Stat roll – TN 15)

          DIVINE
              Page 53 – The Divine Power Pack is typically
          assigned to Attributes that are granted to the character
          by a greater being or higher power. The character
          typically prays to, or otherwise calls upon, the
          divine being in times of need. If the being deems
          the character worthy of attention, it intervenes on the
          character’s behalf by providing access to supernatural
          abilities. This Power Pack is appropriate for clerics,
 PAGE
  74
          priests, shrine maidens, and exorcists … but can also
          be renamed “Infernal” should the granting being
          have demonic rather than divine origins.
            Power Pack: Divine +2
            Activation +1 (Pray for one round)
            Charges +2 (Can only be used three times a day)
            Detectable +1 (Character glows in divine light)
            Potent -3 (Minor edge on Stat rolls, plus 1 re-roll)
            Unique Restriction +1 (Character must keep faith)

          PSI
              Page 90 – The Psi Power Pack is typically
          assigned to Attributes that are powered by the
          character’s raw psychic energy. Through intense
          concentration alone, the character can channel
          their thoughts into waves of reality that can have a
          potent effect on the physical world. This Power Pack
          is appropriate for psionicists, extraterrestrials, psychics,
          and demons.
            Power Pack: Psi +6
            Backlash +1 (If roll fails by margin of 6 or more)
            Concentration +2 (No fighting or complex actions)
            Deplete +1 (-10 Energy Points or -10/minute)
            Detectable +1 (Psionic detection)
            Recovery +1 (Wait 1 minute for every minute of use)
   06: PACKS & BUNDLES
RITUALIST
    Page 25 – The Ritualist Power Pack is typically
assigned to powerful, high-Level Attributes that
involve painstakingly specific preparation before the
power manifests. Such rituals require several assistants
and distinct tools to complete, and carry a very real
probability of disastrous consequences should it not be
performed with care and precision. This Power Pack is
appropriate for necromancers, blood mages, conjurers,
and demonologists.
  Power Pack: Ritualist +7
  Activation +3 (Prepare for 1 hour)
  Assisted +2 (Requires 2-10 helpers)
  Backlash +2 (If roll fails by a margin of 3 or more)
  Concentration +1 (Cannot engage in combat)
  Equipment +1 (Requires portable accessories)
  Potent -2 (Major edge on Stat rolls)

SHAMANIST
    Page 71 – The Shamanist Power Pack is typically
assigned to Attributes that are powered by forces of
nature and the internal balance within the character.
Abilities can only manifest when the character is
emotionally invested in the current events, and they
primarily benefit a friend or companion rather than the
                                                             PAGE
character directly. This Power Pack is appropriate for        75
shamans, medicine gurus, druids, and spiritualists.
  Power Pack: Shamanist +4
  Emotional +2 (Strong emotional involvement)
  Environmental +1 (Native or natural environment)
  Imbue +1 (Up to five companions may benefit)

memorisation of arcane phrases and precise movements

spells also require material components to work as well,
which are consumed in the casting. This Power Pack           PACKS A

  CHAPTER 7

ADVENTURING
 ELEMENTS
                          07: ADVENTURING
ADDING CAMPAIGN COLOUR
   You may have created the most wonderful character imaginable, but their greatness has no impact unless they get
out into the world for an adventure.
    This chapter explores situations and topics that may appear during a role-playing adventure. First, we provide details on
health challenges that characters may encounter during a BESM game, such as poison, disease, and deprivation from life-
sustaining necessities. Threat Scores are up next, which provides a way that Game Masters can determine the appropriate
power level for the character group’s NPC opponents. We then discuss incorporating high-powered characters and
artificial intelligences into the game, followed by multiple pages of firearm and vehicle details and customisations. Finally,
we talk about rewarding players with engagement bonuses, and include a “How Do I?...” section of miscellaneous topics.

           GENETIC DISEASES
               Though commonly called “genetic diseases”,
           “genetic disorders” is more accurate terminology to
     NTS




           classify abnormalities that are formed in the genome.
           Such disorders are often passed to children through
           their parents’ genes, or caused by genetic mutations
   LEME




           or phenotype alterations. Unlike poisons and regular
           diseases, genetic disorders are not usually associated
           with the Weapon Attribute; rather, the expressed
 RING E




           disadvantages are best represented by various Defects
           (BESM4, page 155).


           DEPRIVATION
ADVENTU




               Deprivation is the lack of life-sustaining essentials,
           such as food, sleep, rest, shelter, water, and air. It results
           in a deterioration of the character’s health and in some
           situations can eventually be fatal. But unlike normal
           damage, deprivation is often easily reversed provided it
           has not gone too far (it is usually considered to be Stun
           damage; see BESM4, page 141).
               The appropriate Features or Resilient Attribute
           may prevent specific deprivations from occurring.
           Otherwise, a character suffering deprivation loses one
           Health Point every interval, as described in Table-14.
 PAGE
           All deprivation-incurred Health Point loss is considered
  90       incurable and will not recover until the deprivation is
           ended. For example, a character cannot regain Health
           Points lost due to hunger without eating (or being fed,
           if they are too weak to do so), nor regain those lost from
           freezing water until they are rescued from that icy lake.
               For deprivation Health Point loss due to cold
           and lack of oxygen, these damage values present an
           alternate, simpler rule to those provided under the
           Environmental Damage section (page 70). The Game
           Master can decide which rule they prefer to use.




           14              DEPRIVATION
               Type of                        Health Point Loss
               Deprivation                        Interval
               Starvation                         1 per 8 hours
               Hunger (minimal food)                1 per day
               Total Dehydration                  1 per 2 hours
               Partial Dehydration                1 per 8 hours
               Overheating                        1 per 4 hours
               Freezing Cold                       1 per hour
               Freezing Water                     1 per minute
               Suffocation                         1 per round
               Drowning                           1 per second

THREAT SCORES FOR
OPPONENTS
                                                                  The GM should then consider the following factors
                                                              to determine the final Threat Score of the individual or
     One of the most difficult aspects facing the GM          group opponent.
in many role-playing games is presenting opposition           MOVE UP FOR THESE CONDITIONS:
NPCs that are scaled appropriately for the group. Too
                                                              » Two to four characters
strong, and the player characters will be outmatched
and the players will become frustrated. Too weak, and         » Five to seven characters (two moves up)
the adventures risk becoming pedestrian, boring, and          » Eight or more characters (three moves up)
routine. This challenge is magnified in BESM since it         » Characters have game values that exceed benchmarks
is a diverse, open-ended, point-based system that allows        from the BESM core rules (BESM4, page 24)
for near-endless possibilities for both player characters
                                                              » Characters usually have a significant location/
and NPCs.
                                                                setting advantage against opponents
     BESM Extras presents the new concept of Threat
Scores to assist GMs in the creation and presentation         MOVE DOWN FOR THESE CONDITIONS:
of NPC opponents. An individual’s or group’s Threat           »    Two to four opponents
Score is a measure of how much of a challenge they            »    Five to seven opponents (two moves down)
will present to the player characters during a typical
                                                              »    Eight or more opponents (three moves down)
adventure, which considers both combat and non-
combat situations. The higher the opponent’s Threat           »    Opponents usually have a significant location/
Score, the more challenging they will be for the                   setting advantage against characters
player characters. With many varied ability options,           » Opponents have game values that exceed benchmarks
it’s impossible to develop one flawless set of never-fail          from the BESM core rules (BESM4, page 24)
guidelines that are applicable in all situations, but this     » Opponents have been optimised for conflict with
series of benchmarks for GM consideration will help.               the characters
                                                              For example, the GM starts a new gaming campaign            PAGE
THE POWER LEVEL SPECTRUM                                                                                                   91
    Power levels range across a Character Point spectrum,     with player characters created from 60 Character Points
as outlined in Table-01 from the core rules (see              (adventurer power level). Consequently, the baseline
                                                              Threat Score would be the heroic power level – one
BESM4, page 22): sub-human (0-24); human (25-49);
                                                              move up from the character’s power level.
adventurer (50-74); heroic (75-99); mythical (100-149);
superhuman (150-199); superpowered (200-249); and             The GM then modifies this baseline due to the
godlike (250+). Rather than addressing specific Point         following conditions: there are eight player characters

    The mechanical/digital life of AI doesn’t need to           Health Points as a Derived Value but instead as an Item,
be substantially different than biological life of regular      which means the AI’s Health Points are equal to the

example of AI treated as a normal character, with the                                                                          93

Resilience Attribute reflecting the non-biological nature
of the cybernetic body.

  uLACKING A SOUL
    Alternatively, AI can be similar to their biological
counterparts with one notable exception: artificial
lifeforms do not have a soul and consequently do not
have a Soul Stat. This isn’t exactly the same as having
a zero Soul, though; rather, the character’s Soul Stat
does not exist. Consequently, Derived Values ignore
the consideration of the Soul Stat, meaning that the


                                                                                                                                ADVEN
character’s Combat Value is equal to the average of only
Body and Mind Stats, and Health Points are equal to 10
times the character’s Body Stat instead. When making
Stat rolls, the character’s Mind Stat often replaces their
                                                                                                                             TURING

Soul Stat as required.
    Handling AI this way requires additional effort on
behalf of the player and GM when considering the
absence of a Soul Stat, though most game mechanics
remain largely unchanged.

           07: ADVENTURING
                     RIFLE AND SHOTGUN OWNERSHIP
                        Ownership of a sporting or hunting weapon requires
                    a license. To get one, the applicant must attend a short
     NTS




                    safety lecture at their neighbourhood police station,
                    pass a safety examination, and register for the license
                    (which is good for three years).
   LEME




                        This is only the start of the procedure, however, and
                    only qualifies the applicant to purchase air rifles, and
                    air pistols. To own an actual firearm, the would-be gun
 RING E




                    owner must be over 20 (or 18, if they have the support
                    of an official sports organisation). The procedure
                    depends on the type of firearm.
ADVENTU




                        A permit for a shotgun requires additional paperwork
                    be submitted to the police, along with passing a
                    (fairly easy) shooting examination and an exhaustive
                    background check. All of this takes a few months.
                    As usual, individuals with a history of mental illness,
                    criminal records, drug addiction, or alcohol problems
                    will be denied a license.
                        A permit for a rifle requires the same process, with
                    the additional requirement of membership in the
                    National Rifle Association of Japan plus a more difficult
                    skill certification examination. The purchase of a large
                    calibre (medium or heavy) rifle is only permitted if the
                    applicant has either owned a light rifle for at least a year
 PAGE
                    or has owned a shotgun for at least 10 years.
  98                    Each individual firearm purchase requires a
                    preliminary visit to the gun shop to select the weapon,
                    and then another month’s wait while the gun store sends
                    out paperwork to the police. An individual permit is
                    needed for each weapon. After picking up the permit,
                    the gun can finally be taken out of the store. Within two
                    weeks of the initial purchase, the newly-bought gun must
                    then be taken to a police station for a final inspection.
                    Purchase of ammunition requires additional paperwork
                    and police permission. A gun owner is required to keep
                    their weapon in a secure, locked cabinet. Firearms must
                    also be taken to the police station once a year for an
                    annual inspection.
                     PISTOL OWNERSHIP
                        A license for a pistol (for sports shooting only) is
                    very difficult to get. The would-be license-holder must
                    have passed an air pistol test and gain an excellent score
                    in safety and marksmanship. Moreover, the pistol is not
                    licensed for concealed carry, and must be transported
                    in a sealed case when going to or from a competition
                    – and stored at a police station for safekeeping when
                    not in use. The odds of meeting a citizen with a legally
                    registered pistol are extremely low.

           shotguns only.                                                obstacle on the roll to reflect the move’s outrageousness.
                                                                         Failure usually results in a crash or accident with
                                                                         damage delivered to the vehicle and perhaps the driver
                                                                         (see Crashing, page 68). If the character succeeds, the
ADVENTU




                                                                         pursuing vehicle may also attempt the manoeuvre, or
                                                                         may break off the chase.
                                                                             GMs can use the same technique to help slower
                                                                         vehicles (or characters on foot) catch faster ones
                                                                         during chases. If the pursuing character zips through
                                                                         a dangerous short cut, leaps from building to building,
                                                                         or otherwise attempts something clever, they can make
                                                                         up significant distance with a successful Skill roll. If the
                                                                         character fails the roll, the GM should describe some
                                                                         sort of hazard and allow them to make a second Stat or
                                                                         Skill roll to avoid a complete disaster. For example, if a
                                                                         pedestrian steps into the street in front of the character’s
                                                                         car, a successful Skill roll means that the driver swerves
 PAGE
  102
                                                                         in time and crashes into a nearby fruit stand; failing can
                                                                         result in an injured or dead pedestrian.
                                                                             On a lengthier chase, a successful Skill roll related to
                                                                         navigation or area knowledge could allow the character
                                                                         to think of a clever short cut, while a failure could trap
                                                                         the character in a traffic jam or similar delay. The GM
                                                                         can apply edges or obstacles based on how well the
                                                                         character knows the area and how detailed the player
                                                                         describes the tactics their character is using.
                                                                             One useful technique involves the GM keeping
                                                                         a general mental note of relative distances (in metres
                                                                         or km) between combatants or important objectives.
                                                                         For example, if the encounter begins with a couple of
                                                                         SWAT vans 300 metres away from a criminal’s getaway
                                                                         car, and in the first combat round the vans race forward
                                                                         100 metres, then the opponents are now 200 metres
                                                                         from each other. If one vehicle is chasing another, and
                                                                         is 10 kph faster, it will close the distance by roughly
                                                                         10 metres every round. GMs should not worry about
                                                                         exact speeds and distances – a general idea of the overall
                                                                         distances should be sufficient to avoid a bookkeeping
                                                                         nightmare. See Character Movement (BESM4, page
                                                                         191) for more detailed speed and distance information.

 CHAPTER 8

ITEMS AND
WEAPONS
   08: ITEMS & WEAPONS
GEAR, MECHA, DEVICES & MORE
    Chapter 10 of the BESM core book provides a comprehensive array of Weapons and Armour Items with which you can
equip characters across a variety of adventure genres. It also presents a limited selection of other useful Items, ranging
from protective devices, to mecha and vehicles, to adventuring gear. This chapter expands your choices substantially, and
includes game stats and brief descriptions for over 70 Items across six different categories. The listed game stats for each
Item can easily be modified up or down to suit your campaign power level and the players’ visions for their characters.
    Items that have only a single use (such as potions or exploding Weapons) are not normally acquired with Character
Points unless they are regularly replaced throughout the story, such as a cleric replacing healing potions at their temple or
a soldier replacing grenades at the army base once they are used. This guideline helps maintain Character Point balance.


ADVENTURING
GEAR
     Characters need useful and entertaining gear while
exploring the world on their daring adventures. Don’t
let the word “gear” fool you though, since many of these
entries are quite powerful and are normally acquired
through the Item Attribute.

  u3D MANOEUVRING GEAR
    This harness is worn over clothing by elite combat
rangers to provide exceptional movement capabilities
during wilderness and urban battles. The harness
alternatively shoots out and reels in grappling hooks                                                                            PAGE
using compressed air, which helps propel the character                                                                            111

along all three axes of aeriel movement. Exhaust jets are
distributed along the harness to provide additional
momentum and help stabilise the character’s movements.
    To function properly, the harness requires structures
onto which the grappling hooks can temporarily attach
– buildings, trees, rock formations, flying vehicles,
gigantic opponents, etc.

            uMENAGERIE BALL
              This softball-sized hollow ball has a hinged opening
          that functions as a two-way portal to a city-sized, forested
          pocket dimension. The character can attempt to trap
          Neomorphs (see BESM4, page 227) and other creatures
          inside the ball for easy storage and transportation. On
          command, the character can selectively call forth a
          particular creature stored within the ball, ending its
          dimensional captivity early.

            uPORTABLE HOLE
              When a character needs to get to the other side of a
          wall, locked door, or other such barrier, nothing beats
          a portable hole for effectiveness. The hole resembles a
          foldable, paper-thin black circle that can be stretched
          from one to two metres in diameter. Once placed on
          a the side of a barrier, it creates a hole through and
          provides an immediate passage way for characters and
          objects to the other side. The maximum thickness of
          the barrier is limited to one metre, so the portable hole
          can’t be used to pass through super-thick structures such
          as mountains, planets, or large bodies of water.

           uWARDLING DRAGON
             This magical dragon construct is infused with the
         character’s soul to seemingly animate life. The primary
         protection function of the dragon is to absorb attack
         damage directed at the character and convert it to the
         same amount of Energy Points and Health Points; those
         Points are then transferred back to the character. Should
         the wardling drop to zero Health Points, it must be
         healed or repaired (like any damaged Item).
             The dragon can fly and has sharp claws that can inflict
         minor damage should its master’s life need protection in
         combat. The bond to the dragon heightens the precision
         of all the character’s senses as well. While in proximity
         to the wardling, the character can dampen the effects
         of magical Attributes by two Levels. Additionally, they
         gain strong protection against the harmful effects of
         Attributes that have technological sources.

DICELESS BESM
                   09: DICELESS BESM
THE STORY’S THE THING
   BESM’s origins from 1997 were inspired by designer Mark MacKinnon’s favourite role-playing game, Amber Diceless,
based on Roger Zelazny’s evocative fantasy novel series and created by the late Erick Wujcik of Phage Press. Mark’s goal
was to create an anime game system with a core set of light and easy rules that didn’t get in the way of the characterisation
and role-playing. Consequently, it’s only natural that BESM can be adapted relatively easily to also be played as a diceless
(and fully randomless) game.
    This section doesn’t go into all the supporting advice and guidelines for actually running diceless BESM – which in itself
could take an entire book (or two) to explore! – but rather briefly presents the accommodations and changes necessary to
transition BESM from 2d6 to no dice. We provide the framework, but the application is up to you.



RESOLVING ACTIONS                                                 DICELESS COMBAT
   Removing dice from BESM only impacts one                         Examine the following considerations that would
aspect of the game: resolving actions. Such actions              oversee fast and efficient diceless combat:
may be between characters and their environments, or              » There are no game rounds. A single, calculated total
between two or more characters (or groups) opposing                  combat roll comparison determines the outcome of
one another. Since randomness does not play a role                   the entire battle between opponents.
in character creation or damage delivery, all other
mechanical and role-playing aspects remain relatively             » Better attackers have the combat advantage.
unchanged during the game adventures.                             » Inflicting more damage gives an advantage.
                                                                  » Having more Health Points gives an advantage.

      COMMUNITY
        CONTENT PROGRAMME
The Tri-Stat Emporium is a community content programme hosted on
DriveThruRPG and facilitated by Dyskami Publishing Company. The Emporium
allows you to create and sell new digital content for the Tri-Stat System, as
presented in BESM Fourth Edition. Creators can set a fixed price for their work,
    offer it under pay-what-you-want pricing, or distribute it free of charge.
Through the Emporium, anyone can create digital PDF content using the BESM Fourth Edition game
line and Tri-Stat System and sell it on the DriveThruRPG website. You can write adventures, world books,
character/weapon/animal compilations, new powers and abilities, alternate game rules, fiction, setting/
genre materials, enhanced character sheets, artwork — just about anything you want! You set the sales
 price for your content and DriveThruRPG pays you 50% of that price for every sale made on their site.
Although BESM is marketed as an anime and manga role-playing game, your Emporium works do not
need to have any affiliation with anime or manga or any of the setting/genre material presented in
BESM Fourth Edition. BESM’s underlying Tri-Stat System is a universal role-playing system appropriate
   for any genre, setting, or power level – so do not feel like your works must be limited in any way.




 BESM4.life/emporium
